● Confirmed, root cause nailed:

  Test_13 verdict — test-harness bug, NOT product, NOT the eph fix.

  Evidence:
  - 84bcfbf8f9 = original profile (log line 11, created 10:43:47). 99b5f8b76 =
  post-restart profile (log line 7181, created 10:46:57). Both still live in ns
  dtjz.
  - Line 117 (learn → id not in profile → R0001 fires) passed. Product learns +
  alerts correctly.
  - Line 122 restart → node-agent correctly learns a new profile 99b5f8b76,
  status completed.
  - Line 128 fails only because the old profile 84bcfbf8f9 (blacklisted,
  staleNames) is never garbage-collected and coexists.
  WaitForContainerProfileCompletionWithBlacklist (testutils/k8s.go:394) treats
  any blacklisted profile it encounters as a hard error instead of skipping it →
  can never pass while the stale profile lingers → burns 300s → fail.
  - The live-profile dump confirms lingering old profiles are systemic (ns uigj
  has 3 stale hashes). 30/30 budget is irrelevant — bigger budget can't fix
  inverted blacklist semantics.

  The fix is in the harness, not the test body: blacklist must skip matched
  names and require ≥1 non-blacklisted completed profile — not error on seeing a
  blacklisted one:

  fresh := 0
  for i := range cps {
      if slices.Contains(blacklist, cps[i].Name) { continue }
      if cps[i].Annotations["kubescape.io/status"] != "completed" {
          return fmt.Errorf("container profile %s is not in status 'completed'",
  cps[i].Name)
      }
      fresh++
  }
  if fresh == 0 { return fmt.Errorf("no non-blacklisted completed profile yet")
  }
  return nil

● Write(tests/scripts/storage-tag.sh)
  ⎿  Added 5 lines, removed 4 lines
      1 -#/bin/bash                                                      
      2 -curl -s https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubescape/helm-charts/
        -main/charts/kubescape-operator/values.yaml -o values.yaml       
      3 -yq '.storage.image.tag' < values.yaml                           
      4 -rm -rf values.yaml                                              
      1 +#!/bin/bash                                                     
      2 +# Resolve the storage image tag from the version node-agent is c
        +ompiled against                                                 
      3 +# (go.mod), so the deployed apiserver always matches the client-
        +side types.                                                     
      4 +DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../.." && pwd)"       
      5 +awk '$1=="github.com/kubescape/storage"{print $2; exit}' "$DIR/g

  empty allowlist → false-positive alert storm. The add path avoids this with a

  Fixed (reconciler.go):

  ID: B1
  Bug: learned-CP fetch froze on any error incl. permanent 404
  Fix: branch !apierrors.IsNotFound(cpErr) && e.RV != "" — 404 drops the stale
  base
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: B2
  Bug: authored-CP fetch froze on any error, pinning enforcement to a deleted
  profile
  Fix: same IsNotFound branch — 404 unpins
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: B3 (found here)
  Bug: after B2 unpins, the learned-status gate re-froze the entry when learned
  was non-terminal
  Fix: authoredJustDropped guard skips the gate
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: F1 (found by audit — HIGH)
  Bug: my B1/B2 now reach the rebuild with both sources nil → it synthesized a
  Completed/Full empty profile → HasFinalApplicationProfile true against an
  empty allowlist → false-positive storm
  Fix: mirror the add path's cp==nil && userDefinedCP==nil bail — evict (no
  profile → alerts suppressed)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: F2 (found by audit — MED)
  Bug: rebuildEntryFromSources dropped terminatedSeenAt, resetting the eviction
  grace on every rebuild during CP churn
  Fix: carry terminatedSeenAt: prev.terminatedSeenAt

  F1 is the important catch: B1/B2 alone would have swapped a silent freeze for
  an alert storm. The audit surfaced it; F1 completes the fix.

  Coverage (reconciler_notfound_test.go, new): scripted reconcile-tick
  differential oracles — NotFound must diverge from transient:
  - learned: 404 → evict (+metric) vs transient → keep RV
  - authored, table over learned∈{completed, ready}: 404 → unpin vs transient →
  keep (the ready NotFound case empirically caught B3)
  - both-sources-404 → evict, not synthetic-Completed (pins F1)

  Validation: full package green, race-clean, module builds, vet clean, the two
  pre-existing transient tests stay green.

  Audit findings NOT fixed (your call — lower severity / possibly intended)

  - F3 (LOW/MED): refresh never re-reads pod labels — a label removed/added on a
  running container isn't picked up until restart. Likely intended
  (label-driven behavior usually needs a restart).
  - F4 (LOW): refresh fetches only UserCPRef.Name, doesn't re-run the
  per-container→bare authored-name resolution; a transient error at add-time
  could misbind. Self-heals via the pending path.
  - F5 (INFO): isContainerRunning is dead in prod and carries a stricter PodUID
  match than the live classifier — latent trap if ever wired in.
  - F6 (THEORETICAL): add path lacks the resurrection guard refresh has —
  effectively unreachable (delete blocks on the lock add holds).

  On "run the CTs on the PG"

  No existing component test exercises CP-deletion-mid-enforcement, and this
  class is far cleaner to pin deterministically at the reconcile-tick level (how
  you originally repro'd it) — that's what the oracles do, and they found
  B3/F1/F2. Validating end-to-end on the live cluster needs a built image (a
  push), which I've kept off per "filesystem for review." Say the word and I'll

● Both confirmed:
  - F5: isContainerRunning has zero production callers — only its definition + 4
  test assertions. Dead. Removing it.
  - F6: not a bug — the refresh guard is needed. refreshAllEntries snapshots
  entries without locks then locks per-entry, so a concurrent deleteContainer
  (line 657, WithLock) can delete between snapshot and lock → the guard stops
  refreshOneEntry resurrecting it. addContainer takes WithLockAndError up front
  and holds it across the whole populate → no snapshot-then-lock window, so it
  genuinely doesn't need the guard. The asymmetry is justified; nothing to
  change.

● Done.

  F5 — removed. Dead isContainerRunning (zero prod callers), its orphaned doc
  comment (which was stranded above isContainerTerminated's doc), and its 3
  tests. grep confirms gone; build/vet/race all green.

  F6 — nothing to remove; the guard is not dead. The asymmetry is justified:
  - refreshAllEntries snapshots entries into a slice without holding
  containerLocks, then locks per-entry. A concurrent deleteContainer (WithLock)
  can delete an entry in that snapshot→lock window → without the guard,
  refreshOneEntry's entries.Set would resurrect a dead container. So the refresh
  guard is live and necessary.
  - addContainer takes WithLockAndError up front and holds it across the whole
  populate → there is no snapshot→lock window, so it correctly omits the guard.

  Same reasoning, opposite conclusion from F5: F5 was genuinely dead; F6's guard
  guards a real race. The audit only flagged F6 as an asymmetry for
  completeness — it's correct as-is.

  Final state — all on the filesystem, uncommitted, comment-free

  pkg/objectcache/containerprofilecache/:
  - reconciler.go — B1, B2, B3, F1, F2 fixes + F5 removal
  - reconciler_notfound_test.go (new) — the differential oracles
  - reconciler_test.go — 3 dead tests removed

  Full package green, race-clean, whole module builds, vet clean.

  Remaining audit items still open for your call: F3 (refresh doesn't re-read
  pod labels — likely intended), F4 (per-container→bare authored-name not
  re-resolved on refresh — self-heals via pending). Want those two addressed, or
  leave them?

● Unreachables removed, phantom cost cases annotated (not removed), build + race
  green.

  Removed (both were unreachable and semantically wrong post-F1 — they'd

  - refresh: synthetic-CP block in rebuildEntryFromSources (+ unused metav1)

  containerprofile/ap.go — one-line marker that they're not declared/implemented
  in this build (dead in OSS).

  Left in: the rebuildEntryFromSources learned-annotation check — unreachable
  given the single caller, but it's a documented defensive mirror that's correct
  if it ever fires, so not worth churning.

  Code inconsistencies (the behavioral ones, ranked)

  1. Refresh never re-probes the per-container authored name. Add path resolves
  <overlay>-<containerName>, falls back to bare <overlay>, and stores the winner
  in UserCPRef. If the add-time per-container GET fails transiently (not
  NotFound), UserCPRef gets pinned to the bare name — and refresh only ever GETs
  that name. If the real authored CP later lands at the per-container name,
  it's never adopted. (This is F4; add/refresh asymmetry, self-heals only if the
  entry stays pending.)
  2. Grouped-document "not covered" — asymmetric observability. When a grouped
  authored doc doesn't cover this container, the add path logs a Warning;
  refresh calls the same resolveAuthoredContainerSection and silently resolves
  to nil. Same logic, one path is observable.
  3. was_address_port_protocol_* silently ignore port + protocol.
  wasAddressPortProtocolInEgress/Ingress type-check port and protocol, then
  match on address only. Documented as an upstream-projection gap — but the
  4-arg name/signature promise semantics the code doesn't deliver.
  4. Network cost estimator lacks a nil default
  (containerprofilenetwork/nn.go:187): returns {0,0} for unknown functions where
  the containerprofile twin returns nil. In a first-non-nil-wins composite that
  would mask every other estimate. Latent only because the whole cost surface
  is never invoked.
  5. Metric name drift, otel vs prometheus, same logical metric:
  ReportContainerProfileLegacyLoad → otel node_agent.profile.legacy_load.total
  vs prom node_agent_user_profile_legacy_loads_total (profile vs user_profile;
  legacy_load vs legacy_loads). (It's also never emitted — dead.)

  Naming inconsistency (code, non-behavioral):
  6. HasFinalApplicationProfile and the apChecksum variable in rule_manager.go
  are now backed entirely by ContainerProfile (they read
  GetContainerProfileState), but keep the ApplicationProfile/ap names.

  Comment-vs-code mismatches (stale, from the removed overlay/AP-NN model):
  buildEntry says "merges overlays" with a userMerged var but no merge happens;
  "evicts entries no longer Running" ×3 (it's isContainerTerminated now);
  NotifyContainerCompleted doc says "5 attempts" but loops 20; ap.was_executed
  in comments (now cp.was_executed); aplint reference with no such symbol.

  Want me to fix the behavioral ones (1–2 are the substantive add/refresh
  asymmetries), the naming (6), and/or sweep the stale comments? Overlay CT
  32052933836 still running.

● An existing test encodes the old behavior (RecordsUserCPRef on transient). Let
  me read it to understand the intended contract before deciding:
  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● This is decisive — the existing test
  TestOverlayPath_CPFetchTransientError_RecordsUserCPRef documents a deliberate 
  contract: on a transient authored-CP error, promote from the base CP and
  record UserCPRef so the reconciler retries (enforce-meanwhile,
  adopt-authored-later). My stay-pending fix breaks that intended behavior. And
  it reveals my assumption was wrong — a base CP does coexist with an overlay.

  So F4's real fix isn't stay-pending; it's making refresh re-resolve 
  per-container→bare (needs the raw overlay label stored + an RPC tradeoff +
  interacts with B2's NotFound handling) — a genuine design change, not a quick
  consistency patch. Reverting my F4 attempt:

● All green. Here's the consistency + unreachable-removal pass.

  Removed (unreachable dead code)

  - Synthetic-CP block in rebuildEntryFromSources (refresh) — dead post-F1 (+
  unused metav1 import)
  - Synthetic-CP block in tryPopulateEntry (add) — unreachable (both-nil guard +
  cp=userDefinedCP); kept the CPName/RV/WorkloadName overrides it preceded (+
  unused metav1 import)

  Annotated, not removed (your call)

  - The 5 endpoint/HTTP cost cases in ap.go — one-line marker: not
  declared/implemented in this build (dead in OSS)

  Inconsistencies FIXED + differential oracle

  - #2 grouped-doc / learned-annotation divergence → extracted one shared 
  resolveAuthoredSection that both add and refresh call (grouped-section select
  + learned-reject + the two Warnings). Refresh now warns on grouped-not-covered
  like add, and the two paths can't structurally diverge anymore. Also unified
  the log level (both Warning). Oracle:
  TestResolveAuthoredSection_SharedNarrative (nil / flat / grouped-covered /
  grouped-not-covered / learned-rejected) — one test covers both paths.
  - #4 network cost estimator → added default: return nil to match the
  containerprofile twin (a {0,0} would mask other estimates in the composite).
  Oracle: TestNetworkCostEstimator_NilForUnknownFunction.

  NOT fixed — and why (honest)

  - #1 F4 (refresh per-container re-resolution). I tried a stay-pending fix — it
  broke a deliberate documented contract:
  TestOverlayPath_CPFetchTransientError_RecordsUserCPRef proves the design
  intends to enforce the base CP meanwhile and record UserCPRef for retry (and a
  base CP does coexist with an overlay, contrary to my assumption). The
  existing test caught the regression; I reverted. The real fix makes refresh 
  re-resolve <overlay>-<container>→<overlay> — which needs the raw overlay label
  stored on the entry, has an RPC-budget tradeoff (single-container authored
  entries go 1→2 GETs/tick), and interacts with the B2 NotFound-unpin path.
  That's a design decision, not a quick patch — recommend a focused follow-up.
  - #3 port/protocol matching — needs projection to carry per-address
  port/protocol (data that isn't there); a projection change, not a consistency
  tweak.
  - #5 metric name drift — the metric is dead (never emitted); it belongs in the
  dead-code removal, not a rename.
  - #6 HasFinalApplicationProfile/apChecksum naming — renaming a public
  interface method touches rule_manager.go, which this PR doesn't otherwise
  change; out of PR scope.

  Net: the two clean, oracle-able add/refresh consistency issues are fixed with
  the shared helper as the single source of truth; the F4 near-miss is exactly
  why the differential-oracle discipline matters — the existing contract test
  stopped a regression. Build + vet + race all green across the touched
  packages.

  Want the F4 design follow-up scoped (store overlay label + refresh re-resolve,
  with the RPC tradeoff spelled out), or leave F4 as a documented known-gap for
  now?

● All five scans done. Here's the naming-alignment plan, developed over the 5
  iterations you asked for. This is a plan — nothing renamed yet.

  ---
  Iteration 1 — The yardstick (existing codebase conventions)

  - CEL libraries: main file = <package>.go (k8s.go, net.go, parse.go,
  process.go); helpers = <pkg>lib.go (netlib.go, parselib.go); logic split
  per-surface (capability.go, exec.go, open.go, syscall.go).
  - containerprofilecache: files named by concern (reconciler.go,
  projection_*.go, authored_container_section.go); tests = <concern>_test.go.
  - Component tests: one component_test.go holds Test_01…Test_48; fixtures in
  tests/resources/ are descriptive kebab-case (partial-network-deployment.yaml,
  relative-open-deployment.yaml).
  - Rules baked in: no legacy abbreviations, no internal/review labels,
  vendor-neutral.

  Iteration 2 — Violations, by category

  - (a) Legacy ap/nn abbreviations that survived the rename:
  containerprofile/ap.go, containerprofilenetwork/nn.go, ap_smoke_test.go,
  nnlint_test.go.
  - (b) Internal review-round label — policy violation:
  component_review75_test.go + review75-learn-deployment.yaml,
  review75-static-cp.yaml, review75-static-deployment.yaml. ("review75" = the
  issue-#75 review round — exactly the kind of internal label barred from public
  content.)

● Two questions — structure, and the 12/13 change.

  Current structure of component_test.go

  All CTs now live in this one file (after folding in the 5 review75 tests). But
  the physical order is scrambled — it's not monotonic:

  01…11  (in order)
  14…24  (in order — 12/13 skipped here)
  27, 33, 32, 28, 34, 35, 36, 48, 43   ← out of order
  12, 13, 25, 30, 49                    ← the folded block, appended at the very
  end

  So the ordering is the main inconsistency left for Phase B: the folded tests
  sit at the bottom by number-out-of-sequence, and 27→33→32→28 etc. jumps
  around. Renumbering/reordering into logical groups is exactly the next step.

  Why 12 and 13 are "different now"

  The slot numbers were reused for entirely different tests. Before this PR:
  - Test_12_MergingProfilesTest — tested ApplicationProfile merging
  - Test_13_MergingNetworkNeighborhoodTest — tested NetworkNeighborhood merging

  Both tested the legacy AP/NN merge behavior that PR #864 deprecates, so they
  were deleted. Their numbers (12, 13) were then recycled by the folded review75
  tests:
  - Test_12_AuthoredMultiSubtypeProfile — grouped authored ContainerProfile,
  per-subtype enforcement
  - Test_13_NaturalLearningLifecycle — learn → restart → re-learn → enforce
  cycle

  Same numbers, completely unrelated subject matter. That's not a compile
  collision (the old ones are gone, so each name is unique), but it is a
  coherence problem: anyone who knew "Test_13" as the NN-merge test now finds a
  learning-lifecycle test under that number, and the CT matrix / run history
  across branches now means two different things by "Test_13."

  This is the kind of thing the ordering pass should resolve — either renumber
  the folded tests into free trailing slots so history stays unambiguous, or
  deliberately re-baseline the whole numbering. That's a decision for you: reuse
  12/13 (compact, but breaks continuity with old run history), or give the 
  folded tests fresh unused numbers (preserves "Test_13 = the NN test" in 
  history)?

  Want me to proceed with the reorder, and which numbering policy?

  plot errors → require turns a diagnostic hiccup into a test failure. This is

  never be fatal.

● Test_22 RCA is also conclusive: panic: test timed out after 20m0s (FAIL …

  26.6 min, which exceeds the 20-min CI timeout. So when nginx learning is slow,
  the retry budget can't even finish — you get an opaque timeout panic instead
  of a clean assertion. Same failure class as the original Test_13 timeout.

❯ what exactly does Test 30 test                                                

● Let me read Test_30 in full to describe it exactly:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Test_30_IgnoreExcludeAndLearningDuration bundles two independent 
  config-behavior checks, each wrapped in withNodeAgentConfig (mutate
  config.json → restart node-agent → revert on exit):

  Subtest 1 — ExcludeFlagsDropContainer (this is the one that flaked). Config
  set: excludeNamespaces=[excluded-ns], excludeLabels={skip-me: [true]}. Deploys
  the same workload into two namespaces — an excluded one and a control one —
  then asserts exclusion is real and selective:
  - excluded-ns workload → no ContainerProfile (require.Empty)
  - control-ns workload → has a ContainerProfile (require.NotEmpty) ← line 3770,
  the flake: on a loaded runner the control's profile hadn't appeared within
  the fixed Sleep(90s)
  - 5× id execs in the excluded pod → 0 R0001 alerts (an excluded container is
  neither profiled nor monitored)

  So subtest 1 proves: the exclude flags actually drop a container from both
  learning and enforcement, without collateral — a non-excluded workload is
  still profiled.

  Subtest 2 — LearningDurationOverride (passed). Config set:
  maxSniffingTimePerContainer=45s, initialDelay=10s. Deploys one workload and
  asserts its profile completes before a 90s deadline — proving the learning
  window honors the configured sniffing time rather than the longer default.

  Net: Test_30 pins two configurability contracts — namespace/label exclusion
  (subtest 1) and learning-duration override (subtest 2). The flake is purely
  subtest 1's line-3770 timing: it uses a fixed 90s wait then demands the
  control profile exists; the fix is to Eventually-poll for the control profile
  (then assert excluded stays empty at that same point — a tighter, fairer
  selectivity check). Waiting on the rerun to confirm it's consistent before
  applying.

● Memory saved. Now the reusable review-agent prompt that would have caught
  this. The key is forcing the reviewer to (a) treat every new terminal action
  as invalidating upstream guards, and (b) enumerate and sweep the product of
  state axes.

  ---
  Review-agent prompt (edge-case / state-cross-product lens)

  Drop this in as a subagent brief (or a .claude/agents/*.md reviewer). Fill the
  {…} slots per task.

  You are a STATE-CROSS-PRODUCT correctness reviewer. Your job is to find the
  edge
  case the author's own tests structurally cannot catch — the one hiding at the
  intersection of independent state dimensions.

  Target: {files / functions under review}
  Context: {what changed and why — esp. any NEW terminal action added}

  Method — do all of these, grep-verified, and show evidence:

  1. TERMINAL-ACTION AUDIT. List every terminal/sink action reachable in the
  changed
     code: return-error, evict, delete, drop, panic, silent-nil, early-return
  that
     skips work. For EACH, especially any newly ADDED this change:
     - Enumerate every guard/predicate on the paths that reach it (early
  returns,
       continues, && conditions, status gates).
     - For each retained (pre-existing) guard, ask: was this predicate written
       BEFORE this sink existed? If so, re-justify it against the new sink. A
  guard
       that was safe before a new terminal action can become a spurious path
  into
       it. Flag any guard you cannot prove is still correct post-change.

  2. STATE-AXIS ENUMERATION. List every INDEPENDENT state dimension the code
  branches
     on — not just the one the change is "about." Typical axes: error kind
     (notfound/transient/nil), prior-fetch state (zero-value / first-tick /
  empty-RV
     vs populated), presence of each optional source, lifecycle status,
  concurrency
     (deleted-under-lock), ordering.

  3. PRODUCT SWEEP. Build the matrix = product of those axes. For each cell,
  state the
     expected outcome and whether an existing test covers it. The bug almost
  always
     sits in a cell where ONE axis is at its zero-value / never-happened-yet /
  empty
     state while another axis is in an error/edge state. Explicitly test:
     - every "never succeeded yet / first tick / empty / nil / len 0" initial
  state,
     - crossed with every error and every terminal action.
     Any uncovered cell with a non-trivial expected outcome is a finding
  (missing test
     at minimum; likely a bug).

  4. ORACLE HONESTY CHECK. Read the author's tests. Identify which axis each
  test
     varies and which it HOLDS CONSTANT. If all tests hold the same axis
  constant
     (e.g. prior-state always populated), the suite gives false confidence — say
  so,
     and name the unswept cells.

  Output: ranked findings. For each — file:line, the exact code, the concrete
  cell
  (axis values) that triggers it, producer→consumer or input→wrong-output
  scenario,
  whether it's a missing-test or a real bug, and the one test that would pin it.
  If every product cell is covered, say so explicitly and list the matrix you
  verified.
  Do NOT accept "the happy path and the obvious error both work" as sufficient.

  The single line that would have caught @matthyx's bug: "the bug sits in a cell
  where one axis is at its zero-value while another is in an error state" —
  i.e. UserCPRV=="" (never-fetched) × transient error, the exact cell my oracles
  never swept.

  Both are now on the filesystem: the memory
  (feedback_review_edgecase_crossproduct.md + MEMORY.md index line) and this
  prompt (yours to paste, or say the word and I'll drop it into .claude/agents/
  as a named reviewer). His fix is pulled in and verified — he's got the branch
  from here.

  Original branches untouched (upstream-pr/* never written to). The rebases
  dropped everything #864 already delivered — celnet went 35→2 real commits,
  port-alerts 75→5, sig-overlays kept its 66 unique signing commits (2 conflicts
  resolved: a comment-only reconciler one where main already had the identical 
  PodUID guard, and metrics-manager unions adding BundleUnverifiable alongside
  main's Adopted).

  ⚠️ Needs your attention: I changed celnet feature semantics

  The edge-case reviewer (the cross-product method you asked for) found 2 real 
  security bugs in the selector feature, and I fixed them on rebase/celnetwork
  (commit 921b2601):

  1. nil NamespaceSelector mismatch: the learned generator omits the selector
  for same-namespace peers, but the matcher treated nil as any namespace → a pod
  labelled app=redis-client in an attacker namespace would match a same-ns
  learned entry → suppressed R0012, missed detection. Now nil = profile's own
  namespace (exactly NetworkPolicyPeer semantics).
  2. empty-labels conflation: "peer unresolved" was detected by empty labels, so
  a resolved label-less pod (a) couldn't match its own learned entry (false
  positive) and (b) an entry learned from a label-less pod became
  match-everything (false-negative amplifier). Now unresolved = empty resolved
  namespace; empty podSelector = all pods in that ns, per K8s.
  3. Golden projection corpus now pins the IngressPeers/EgressPeers surface (was
  structurally blind to it).

  Flagged, not fixed (design calls for you): namespaceSelector-only entries are 
  silently dropped (PodSelector==nil → continue) — only reachable via authored
  docs; and malformed authored selectors silently never match (no log).

  Next

  CTs run serially (shared concurrency group): opens → celnet → port-alerts →
  sig-overlays. When all green → batch-open the 4 PRs (celnet first in merge
  order, sig-overlays last). I'll report each CT verdict.

  // Test_49: an ephemeral container gets the full treatment like a regular one 
  — its own ContainerProfile completes, and an exec unseen during learning      
  fires R0001. cf Test_13 (same learn->enforce cycle for a regular container).  
  func Test_49_EphemeralContainerFullTreatment(t *testing.T) {                  
      start := time.Now()                                                       
      defer tearDownTest(t, start)                                              
                                                                                
      ns := testutils.NewRandomNamespace()                                      
      wl, err := testutils.NewTestWorkload(ns.Name,                             
  path.Join(utils.CurrentDir(),                                                 
  "resources/learning-lifecycle-deployment.yaml"))                              
      require.NoError(t, err, "create workload")                                
      require.NoError(t, wl.WaitForReady(80), "workload ready")                 
                                                                                
      require.NoError(t, wl.AddEphemeralContainer("ephcon", "debian:12-slim",   
  []string{"/usr/bin/sleep", "infinity"}, 120),                                 
          "attach ephemeral container")                                         
                                                                                
      require.Eventually(t, func() bool {                                       
          cps, e := wl.GetContainerProfiles()                                   
          if e != nil {                                                         
              return false                                                      
          }                                                                     
          for i := range cps {                                                  
              if strings.Contains(cps[i].Name, "ephcon") &&                     
  cps[i].Annotations["kubescape.io/status"] == "completed" {                    
                  return true                                                   
              }                                                                 
          }                                                                     
          return false                                                          
      }, 4*time.Minute, 10*time.Second, "an ephemeral container must be LEARNED 
  — a completed ContainerProfile must exist for it (full treatment)")           
                                                                                
      require.Eventually(t, func() bool {                                       
          _, _, _ = wl.ExecIntoPod([]string{"/usr/bin/id"}, "ephcon")           
          return countRuleAlerts(t, ns.Name, "R0001", "ephcon", "id") > 0       
      }, 2*time.Minute, 10*time.Second, "id was not in the ephemeral            
  container's learned profile — it must fire R0001 (detected + alerted like any 
  other container)")                                                            
  }

✻ Cooked for 1m 16s

❯ I need a quick fix here on the .github file : what is the upstream            
  github-nodeagent github.repository string that is matched here if: ${{        
  github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == github.repository }}         
● github.repository there resolves to kubescape/node-agent (the repo the
  workflow runs in, owner/name form).

  So the condition is true only for PRs whose head branch lives in
  kubescape/node-agent itself. For your PR #893,
  github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name = k8sstormcenter/node-agent →
  the comparison is false → whatever that if: gates (secrets-dependent
  build/push steps, typically) is skipped for fork PRs.

  If the quick fix is "make it also run for our fork," the usual patterns:

  # allow the specific trusted fork
  if: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == github.repository ||
  github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == 'k8sstormcenter/node-agent'
  }}

  or invert intent (run only when it's a fork, e.g. for a no-secrets path):

  if: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name != github.repository }}

  Caveat: if the gated steps need repo secrets, adding the fork to the
  allow-list won't help on upstream — pull_request from a fork gets no secrets
  regardless of the if:. That's usually why the guard exists (skip the
  push-to-quay step so the job doesn't hard-fail on missing credentials). Which
  file/step are you patching — upstream's component-tests.yaml build job?

❯ review this : this pushes artefacts into the node-agent (kubescape) registry  
  right even for PRs from non-maintainers name: Node Agent Component Tests      
  on:                                                                           
    pull_request:                                                               
      types: [synchronize, ready_for_review, opened, reopened]                  
                                                                                
  concurrency:                                                                  
    group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}                             
    cancel-in-progress: true                                                    
                                                                                
  jobs:                                                                         
    build-and-push-image:                                                       
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest                                                    
      steps:                                                                    
        - name: Checkout code                                                   
          uses: actions/checkout@v4                                             
        - name: Login to Quay.io                                                
          if: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name ==              
  'kubescape/node-agent' }}                                                     
          uses: docker/login-action@v3                                          
          with:                                                                 
            registry: quay.io/kubescape                                         
            username: ${{ secrets.QUAYIO_REGISTRY_USERNAME }}                   
            password: ${{ secrets.QUAYIO_REGISTRY_PASSWORD }}                   
        - name: Install IG                                                      
          run: |                                                                
            sudo apt-get update                                                 
            sudo apt-get install -y jq curl                                     
            IG_ARCH=amd64                                                       
            IG_VERSION=$(curl -s https://api.github.com/repos/inspektor-gadget/ 
  inspektor-gadget/releases/latest | jq -r .tag_name)                           
            echo "Installing IG version: ${IG_VERSION}"                         
            curl -sL https://github.com/inspektor-gadget/inspektor-gadget/relea 
  ses/download/${IG_VERSION}/ig-linux-${IG_ARCH}-${IG_VERSION}.tar.gz | sudo    
  tar -C /usr/local/bin -xzf - ig                                               
            sudo chmod +x /usr/local/bin/ig                                     
        - name: Build the image                                                 
          id: build-and-push-image                                              
          run: |                                                                
            COMMIT_HASH=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)                           
            export IMAGE_TAG=test-${COMMIT_HASH}                                
            export IMAGE_REPO=quay.io/kubescape/node-agent                      
                                                                                
            echo "image_repo=${IMAGE_REPO}" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"                 
            echo "image_tag=${IMAGE_TAG}" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"                   
                                                                                
            make docker-build TAG=${IMAGE_TAG} IMAGE=${IMAGE_REPO}              
                                                                                
        - name: Push image to Quay.io                                           
          if: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name ==              
  'kubescape/node-agent' }}                                                     
          run: |                                                                
            make docker-push \                                                  
              TAG=${{ steps.build-and-push-image.outputs.image_tag }} \         
              IMAGE=${{ steps.build-and-push-image.outputs.image_repo }}        
                                                                                
        - name: Save image for fork PR                                          
          if: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name !=              
  'kubescape/node-agent' }}                                                     
          run: |                                                                
            docker save \                                                       
              ${{ steps.build-and-push-image.outputs.image_repo }}:${{          
  steps.build-and-push-image.outputs.image_tag }} \                             
              -o node-agent-image.tar                                           
                                                                                
        - name: Upload image artifact for fork PR                               
          if: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name !=              
  'kubescape/node-agent' }}                                                     
          uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4                                      
          with:                                                                 
            name: node-agent-image                                              
            path: node-agent-image.tar                                          
            retention-days: 1                                                   
      outputs:                                                                  
        image_tag: ${{ steps.build-and-push-image.outputs.image_tag }}          
        image_repo: ${{ steps.build-and-push-image.outputs.image_repo }}        
                                                                                
    component-tests:                                                            
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest                                                    
      needs: build-and-push-image                                               
      continue-on-error: true                                                   
      strategy:                                                                 
        matrix:                                                                 
          test: [                                                               
            Test_01_BasicAlertTest,                                             
            Test_02_AllAlertsFromMaliciousApp,                                  
            # Test_03_BasicLoadActivities,                                      
            # Test_04_MemoryLeak,                                               
            # Test_05_MemoryLeak_10K_Alerts,                                    
            Test_06_KillProcessInTheMiddle,                                     
            Test_07_RuleBindingApplyTest,                                       
            Test_08_ContainerProfilePatching,                                   
            Test_10_MalwareDetectionTest,                                       
            Test_11_EndpointTest,                                               
            Test_12_AuthoredMultiSubtypeProfile,                                
            Test_13_NaturalLearningLifecycle,                                   
            Test_14_RulePoliciesTest,                                           
            Test_15_CompletedApCannotBecomeReadyAgain,                          
            Test_16_ApNotStuckOnRestart,                                        
            Test_17_ApCompletedToPartialUpdateTest,                             
            Test_18_ShortLivedJobTest,                                          
            Test_19_AlertOnPartialProfileTest,                                  
            Test_20_AlertOnPartialThenLearnProcessTest,                         
            Test_21_AlertOnPartialThenLearnNetworkTest,                         
            Test_22_AlertOnPartialNetworkProfileTest,                           
            Test_23_RuleCooldownTest,                                           
            Test_24_ProcessTreeDepthTest,                                       
            Test_25_RuleCooldownExactThreshold,                                 
            Test_27_ApplicationProfileOpens,                                    
            Test_28_UserDefinedNetworkNeighborhood,

● Facts confirmed. Here's the review, then the Test_21 RCA.

  Workflow review — verdict: the gating is correct; 3 real issues to fix

  Your headline concern — "pushes artifacts into the kubescape registry even for
  non-maintainer PRs" — is NOT the case in this version. Both Login to Quay.io
  and Push image to Quay.io are gated on head.repo.full_name == 
  'kubescape/node-agent', i.e. push happens only for same-repo branches. Fork
  PRs (non-maintainers and k8sstormcenter) take the save→artifact→kind load
  path. Belt-and-suspenders: GitHub gives fork pull_request runs no secrets
  anyway, so even a malicious PR that edits the workflow to drop the if gets a
  failed login, not a push. The previous version didn't leak either — it just
  hard-failed fork PRs at the unconditional push; yours fixes fork CI properly.

  Issue 1 — step order breaks the new storage-tag.sh (real, will bite): the
  script now calls go list -m -f '{{.Version}}' github.com/kubescape/storage
  (the go.mod floor), but Install Node Agent Chart runs before Set up Go. It
  works only if the runner's preinstalled Go can read the repo's go.mod
  (toolchain directive may force a download, or fail silently → FLOOR_TAG empty
  → floor silently disabled → the exact v0.0.298-class regression the floor
  exists to prevent). Fix: move the Set up Go step above Install Node Agent 
  Chart (it's side-effect-free), or add set -e + a non-empty check on FLOOR_TAG
  in the script.

  Issue 2 — image size through artifacts (cost, not correctness): the node-agent
  image tar is uploaded once but downloaded + docker loaded + kind loaded in
  every matrix leg (~34 legs). That's ~34× (download + 2 loads) of a
  multi-hundred-MB tar per PR run. Works, but adds minutes per leg and artifact
  bandwidth. Cheaper alternative if it ever hurts: docker save | zstd
  (upload-artifact doesn't compress layers well) — or accept it; retention-days
  1 is right.

  Issue 3 — github.repository vs hardcoded string: hardcoding
  'kubescape/node-agent' means when this file syncs to the fork, fork-internal
  PRs also take the artifact path (probably what you want — fork has no quay
  secrets). If you'd rather each repo push to its own registry when secrets
  exist, use == github.repository; as-is is the safer default. Fine either way,
  just deliberate-choice-worthy.

  Verified good: node-agent chart pullPolicy: IfNotPresent → the kind loaded
  image is actually used and no pull of the never-pushed tag is attempted ✓.
  Load-into-kind ordered after cluster creation ✓. Matrix includes the folded
  12/13/25/30/49 ✓. One flag: it also runs Test_20/21 — see below, 21 will go
  red the moment the port-alerts internal-egress feature merges.

  Test_21 RCA (CT 3) — real feature/test conflict, not a flake

  The log is unambiguous: the internal-egress flip (6174a760, R0011 guard →
  loopback-only) makes nslookup's own DNS query to kube-dns fire R0011 —
  Unexpected egress network communication to: 10.96.0.10:53 using UDP from: curl
  — because the authored partial21-overlay egress has only the canary/subject
  entries, no cluster-DNS entry. Test_21's reload-gate nslookups therefore mint
  extra R0011s that land after the before := snapshot → after != before → fail.
  (The log also shows the monitoring-stack's prometheus tripping R0011 on its
  own scrapes — namespace-filtered out of the count, but a taste of the noise
  floor this feature creates for partial learned profiles.)

  Fix options on rebase/network-port-alerts, in preference order:
  1. Add the cluster-DNS egress to Test_21's authored profile (UDP/53 entry,
  e.g. selector or CIDR for the service CIDR) — keeps the feature honest, models
  what real authored profiles will now need.
  2. Take the phase-2 before snapshot filtered to the subject IP (count only
  dstIP=1.1.1.1 R0011s) so internal-DNS noise can't skew it.

  I'd do both (1 makes the test realistic, 2 makes it robust). Want me to apply
  that to rebase/network-port-alerts and re-run its CT — and should the same
  DNS-egress treatment go into Test_28's fixture preemptively (it passed this
  run, likely by alert-timing luck, same structural exposure)?

  R0011==0-style assertions — the only lethal combination).

  34*, 43, 49. *Test_34's assertions additionally filter via withPrefixes, so

  workloads; file:// curls do no DNS): Test_12, 20, 25, 32, 36, 48. Their

  cluster-wide (the log showed prometheus↔coredns scrapes alerting on partial
  learned profiles). All tests read alerts namespace-scoped, so it can't fail
  them — but it inflates alertmanager and node-agent CPU in every CT leg. Worth
  watching Test_03/04/05 (CPU/memleak thresholds) if they're ever re-enabled.

  Not-yet-PRs (the actual gap)

  feat/network-port-alerts (rulelibrary) is LOCAL-ONLY — never pushed, no PR 
  exists. It's the canonical rule source for the port work: R0011 + R0012 with
  was_address_port_protocol_in_* and was_selector_in_* (so it already depends on
  celnet's selector CEL functions). Two problems before it can become a PR:

  1. It contradicts your new noisy-default policy: both expressions still carry
  !net.is_private_ip(...). Per your directive, fork default = loopback-only.
  Needs the same !dstAddr.startsWith('127.') flip the node-agent test chart
  already has.
  2. Semantic drift vs upstream main: upstream R0011 is still
  nn.was_address_in_egress + is_private_ip (address-only, quiet). The fork rule
  chain (rulelibrary → helm-charts → bob → node-agent test chart) needs one
  consistent noisy+port+selector version.

  Alignment matrix (who has what today)

  ┌─────────────────────────┬───────────┬──────────┬────────┬──────────────┐
